## Live checks

## Spec decisions
- MODULES §F: money in smallest unit + inventory.currency query, ctx.rev, query tools, REFUSED, payloads, equipment stacks, one fight pays one quest, damage effects.

## Integration notes (to apply when combining)
- RB-trade: 'no market' / 'not ready' use INVALID → switch to REFUSED (MODULES §F4). calls.js holds all cross-module payloads.
- RB-trade: ready:null = collectable any time when there is no story clock (kept, v1 behaviour).
- RB-items: refusals use INVALID → REFUSED for rule refusals. Hooks read settings via sdk.settings fallback.
- SD-quests: wallet.receive sends a coin word; convert to smallest unit via inventory.currency (§F1); add beaten:<rev> claims (§F7).
- Queries get (state, args, tools) — trade calls it env {settings, query, hook}; unify at integration.
- SB-fishing: grant happens in fishing.reel (cast decides catch+timing from its seed; reel takes the press flag, no randomness). Root package.json must add examples/fishing/tests/*.test.js and check.mjs should cover examples/. inventory.grant must keep explicit rarity/value (item.define must not overwrite) — confirm in SB-domains + RB-items.
- SB-layouts: bare screen trees accepted; payloads can't bind queries (only $item) — consider allowing; evaluate scope {item,index,query}. Widget names namespaced (§F9), themes per §F10.
- RB-combat: party.applyResult payload {outcome, stakes, actors:[{id,hp,mp,sp,down,conditions:[{kind,rounds}],uses}]} must match RB-party; conditions naming (poison vs poisoned) to unify. inventory.grant with full story def: host must run item.define on unknown defs and keep existing def ids (and keep explicit rarity/value, per fishing). Summary still uses v1 default currency → switch to inventory.currency. Plan stages join as waves for now.
- SB-events: events use calls only (query('party.pc').level). turn.js must await beforeGenerate hooks. settings: section title + render(el, api) — module.js reads both. packs.content({kind:'events'}) assumed a flat array — check SB-packs. Condition parser supports names/calls options for template expressions (§13.2) — SB-templates should import it.
- RB-party: needs ctx.carrier (reply key) for one-blow-per-reply and ctx.id(prefix) — add both to the bus at integration. Settings keys flat — namespace per part at module.js merge. applyResult takes {result: v1 summarize} vs combat's {outcome, actors…} — reconcile (resumed agent was told combat's shape). canGenerate hook requested (§F12).
- NSD: add npc.portrait query (§F11); scripts/package.py support_files += src/storysuite-v2.js; v2 host must not also offer v1 registerTab/StorySuite overlay to NSD.
- User decisions recorded: one currency (§F1), one fight per kill quest (§F7), portraits (§F11), mandatory creation (§F12), no model-written state blocks (§F13), merges wait for user, v2 never into main without go.
- SB-mounts: renderer scope {state,q,draft,settings,changes,mount,index,screenId,api}; mounts call query(name,args,state) → adapt to (state,args,tools). Load mounts.css via manifest/suite CSS. Store needs changesAt(index) → paths from carrier ops. Reader choices storage key: extension_settings.storySuite.screens/cards.
- CAPTURE CTX (needed by tracker, party, quests): bus.run for capture must expose ctx.carrier (reply index + swipe key), ctx.chat = { lastUser (the reader's message text), reply (the reply text) }, ctx.rev, ctx.id(prefix). Tracker counts turns per Director-line reply until then. {{user}} substitution in static/tail by host. Tracker could set taken:{rev} when the reader's words accept (decide: yes, so typed acceptance counts kills from there). Calendar settings use type 'group'.

## Wave 4 (after the wiring passes and SB integration merge) — from F28–F77
- RB: per-member auto/manual spend (F29), ≤2 checks/reply (F32), checks resolve automatically at capture with code-chosen modifiers, no reader edits, pending card removed or read-only (F78 supersedes F16/F33), market shortage kind (F36), carry limit with toggle (F39), party size setting (F48), export/import character (F66), versions individual (F68).
- SB: missing-<Changes> warning + Re-read (F40), refused lines in changes card (F41), Alt+G game view (F53), status strip default on (F54), UI strings centralised (F56), reduced motion (F57), backup-interval guide (F65), REVIEW.md (F74), bug template (F73), v1 branch kept (F70).
- Then storage tuning (PERFORMANCE.md), then alpha tag, then Studio (F75–F77).
- RB QA (#75) findings → wave 4 RB fixes (coordinator decisions):
  * combat.end must always settle: grantXp to a capped member is a no-op (ok, 0), not REFUSED; actors gone are skipped; consumables are consumed AT USE inside the fight (combat.act → inventory.consume), so no stack is double-counted or missing at the end; while a fight runs, party.removeCompanion and party.create{replace} are REFUSED.
  * removed/replaced actors' stacks and wallet go back to the party bag.
  * accessories: RB item.equip uses distinct slot keys per allowed copy (accessory-1, accessory-2) so SB's one-per-slot rule holds; the receipt says what came off when something is displaced.
  * party.grantXp with an unknown difficulty → INVALID.
- SB fixes (wave 4): wallet.receive/pay from a ui cause need a caller module (same rule as inventory.grant); currency plural (setting name singular + optional plural, formatAmount '1 mark', '44 marks'); an item named exactly the currency becomes wallet coin.
- SB QA (#70) findings. Hardening (packs/template/css/screens theme) → SB-harden agent (running, PR into v2-int or v2).
  Core fixes (SB-core-fix agent AFTER the integration merges), coordinator decisions:
  * store replay skips only the failing receipt span, not the whole carrier (later UI receipts + claims survive a middle delete) — bug 1.
  * a failed save before a generation must not drop the turn: keep going with in-memory state, mark dirty, retry after — bug 2.
  * internal commands (`capture.*`, `module.init.*`, `events.fire`) refuse cause 'ui' and screens/widgets can't name them — bugs 3–5.
  * sdk.command options can't raise cause above 'ui' from screens/widgets; host code uses the bus — spec Q.
  * checkOps refuses __proto__/constructor/prototype keys in values — bug 6; capture labels use null-prototype objects — bug 7.
  * implement §3.3 save verification (read back head rev) — spec Q.
- F80 checks (wave 4 RB-checks rework): pre-rolled d20s per reply/swipe in the tail (reuse v1 SB src/dice.js rollDice/promptRolls, GPL-3.0 note kept), code-built modifier table in the tail, `? check … | roll: N | result: …` captured, code recomputes and records, mismatch flagged on the card; remove pending/manual roll flow; ≤2 checks.
- RB wire (#76) merged. Follow-ups: SB packs `terms` content kind ([{pack, levelName, xpName, tiers}]); board widget + status card use party.terms; combat.end skip any gone member (QA); sum()/stepper pool host helper; drop party's unused hand-built tab code.

- RB REBUILD (after beta.2 and the quota reset), USER decisions:
  - SD = full GM: world, time, NPCs, arcs, narrative and non-combat checks. RB = rules: Soul Class 1–4 class system (pack-defined trees, permanent path choice, past SC4 only stat mastery), skills inherited along the path with 3 active, the same 6 stats, 8 slots, 2H and dual wield, stat requirements on gear instead of class limits, companions with full sheets and an AI/manual toggle (party = PC + 2 active), weight encumbrance, combat (kept) and its maths, crafting, trade, QUESTS (moved from SD, so SD stays genre-neutral), SAVE POINTS (moved from SB), XP/levels. SB = inventory plus the hub.
  - Fixed structure only; content stays generative and validated by code. Fresh start; the standard pack doubles as the Studio baseline.
  - Skills per pathway: Classless 3→9→27 (39), then Primary, Intermediate and Advanced 9→27 (36 each) = 147 per pathway, about 1,443 per pack across all 40 classes. Content scale is a concern: proposed base skills plus variant rules.
- RB rebuild, round 2 (USER):
  - Save points stay in SB.
  - SD = genre-neutral goal/thread tracking; RB upgrades an accepted thread into a quest (rewards, XP, requirements, turn-in).
  - SD gets its own roll structure and settings, independent of RB; RB optionally supplies stat modifiers by query.
  - Generated content is bounded by lore and area: the area's danger tier (SD world) sets the foe pool and power budget, and RB clamps.
  - Companion AI is code. Each skill declares its scaling stat.
  - Proposed: a skill lattice (3 choices per step drawn from shared per-tier pools) and/or variant rules to bound node count; "each class gives 3 skills, skills evolve along their own 3-choice pathways" (USER option 5) under discussion.
- RB rebuild, round 3 (USER): level cap 99. Soul Class choices at levels 0/10/20/30 (basic/primary/intermediate/advanced), each class giving its 3 skills. From 40, every 10 levels: one pathway step for one skill (3 choices), each skill progressing twice, so 6 steps (40–90) and every skill reaches its final form. Proposal under discussion: of each step's 3 choices, 1 is the straight power-up (generated) and 2 are branches (written), about 24 written + 12 generated per advanced class, ~650 written per pack.
- RB rebuild, round 4 (USER): promotion (10/20/30) needs the level AND all 3 class skills at 5/5; pathway steps after 40 also need the current form at 5/5. XP keeps flowing while waiting. Skill uses come from combat, from narrative use (code-validated lines), and from training (a time-skip with a trainer, events). Caps per reply and per story day; trainers cost money and time (SD clock). The Party card shows the promotion checklist.
- RB rebuild, round 5 (USER):
  - (1) Factions and reputation = an optional SD feature (genre-neutral).
  - (2) Summons and pets are temporary combat units.
  - (3) Permanent death in narrative and battle (companions; guard: code decides death).
  - (4) Stat points are player-chosen.
  - (5) Rarity: random up to epic by area tier; legendary only from packs; unique named items allowed.
  - (6) Money sinks included (mostly narrative, deducted by validated lines).
  - (7) All quest types (bounty, repeatable, timed, chains), expiring on SD's clock.
  - (8) Difficulty setting in RB if cheap.
  - (9) Crafting open to anyone, scaled by stats.
  - (10) Level-ups, promotions and skill steps are narrated (tail line).
  - (11) No respec, ever.
  - (12) RB prompt part varies with the situation under an adjustable cap.
  Seams agreed in principle: companion sheet RB / relationship NSD; shop RB keyed by SD place; SD owns the clock (RB asks); item defs RB/pack, stacks SB.
- Death (USER): in battle, HP 0 = downed, not dead. In narrative, a lethal event = dead or gravely injured unless an intervening event saves them, decided by a code-run check. PC death = back to the last save point (SB), as if nothing happened (companions alive again). Proposed: a party wipe counts as PC death; a downed companion recovers after the fight with an injury condition.
- Death, round 2 (USER): manual saves plus automatic saves at danger points (like MVU's 'phoenix pact'). On PC death the story continues: the state is restored to the save point and the narrator writes a waking-at-the-save-scene passage; the chat history is kept (a faded-timeline marker). Open: does the character remember (déjà vu) or not?
- Death, round 3 (USER): déjà vu by default (a vague, subtle sense; no exact knowledge), with a setting for no memory; full memory (time loop) as a pack option. The RB rebuild design is complete enough for phase 0 (contract + pack schema) after beta.2 and the quota reset.
- RB rebuild, how it is built (USER): Studio is written by Codex (Sol, on the VM) to mocks approved in the concept artifact, using SB's widgets and tokens; Sonnet reviews each diff (Sol does not review its own code); the lead merges. After phase 0 (contract, pack schema v2 incl. state shapes, CI matrix; the lead) four tracks run in parallel, each owning its own folders:
  - A. Moving code: quests SD→RB, money SB→RB, NSD preset into NSD, the 8 standard slots (Haiku, or DeepSeek non-thinking if its trial passes).
  - B. Rules core: Soul Class tiers, pathways and power budget, 5/5 caps, training, promotion, combat on the new class model, companions and summons, death and return to save. Writer: Sol via Codex (trial on the 5/5 cap + promotion checks first) or Sonnet.
  - C. RB UI: built against the contract's state shapes with fixture states; wired to B at the end.
  - D. Studio (Codex).
  Pack content is written early to the schema and balanced after B. Shared files (module registration, settings, CHANGELOG) belong to the lead, who stitches them at merge time. Merge order: phase 0 → A → B → C wiring → integration/CI matrix → user test. Estimate: about 6–8 working days.

- USER: Story Studio is a standalone app, not a SillyTavern extension: users download it and run it locally. LEAD consequences:
  (1) ship a single self-contained file, dist/story-studio.html, built by a bundling step so it works when opened from file:// (part 1's dev page needs a server or a browser flag because module scripts are blocked on file://), plus `npm start` for a local server and development;
  (2) AI connection = the creator's own endpoint and key, entered in the app and kept only in that browser, never in exported packs or logs; works with OpenAI-compatible and local servers; browser CORS limits documented per provider;
  (3) ST hand-off by files only: export a pack file, or a character card with the pack inside, which SB offers to install when the card is imported; extension settings travel as pack-suggested defaults the reader approves; Studio never edits ST directly;
  (4) dev mode for module makers through the local server, or the browser's folder access where supported.
- USER: use MVU Game Maker (github.com/KritBlade/MVU_Game_Maker) as the reference for Studio. What it is: a ZIP holding a single 1.5 MB dist/index.html you double-click; load an existing ST card → pick a genre template → export JSON → "Download PNG card" (card data embedded in a default image) → import into ST. The GUI is customised in a separate companion, the drag-and-drop "Status Menu Builder". No AI connection in the app. Its rules run as lorebook JavaScript (Tavern Helper/MVU) and the model enforces them, at about 75k tokens a reply. Licence: AGPL-3.0 code and CC BY-NC-SA content → ideas only, never copy its code or content into our GPL-3.0 repos.
  Studio adopts:
  - one double-click HTML;
  - load a card → template → export pack file or PNG card;
  - a drag-and-drop screen builder over SB's layouts kind and widget registry, with live preview.
  Studio differs:
  - data-only packs and module code as the referee, not lorebook scripts and model-enforced rules (cheap prompts; no frontier model needed);
  - an optional AI assist on the creator's own key.

- 28 Oct — USER on custom blocks: (1) can readers type their own fields (a customizable block)?; (2) data modules are the way — any inline editing?; (3) yes to a bounded growing list in data modules. Side thought: RB's downfall was forcing quests into structure; a narrative-only quest (AI handles it, no XP/rewards) is more direct. Lead: (1) yes — a "Custom tracker" builder that writes an ordinary data-module definition (same validator; changes after play are additive, like pack migration); (2) yes — data modules already allow reader actions from a screen (`dm.act`, kinds with `ui: true`), recorded like Inventory's Drop; a generic per-field Edit (tap value → type/pick → Save) fits that; (3) noted; quests: agreed — a story-only quest list (taken / notes / done / failed, no pay, no templates) is the same bounded-list feature (or an SD "story-only quests" switch).
- 28 Oct — USER: so SB/SD can manage nearly all of the quest system; can SB inventory or a custom data module hold assets and locations (bases)?; can a lean text-based combat run in SB/SD? Lead: quests — yes (SD's Quests tab already runs without RB; a story-only mode/list removes pay and templates). Assets — single owned things fit inventory today (key items with notes); a base with its own state (level, upgrades, staff, upkeep) fits a data module (one fixed-field record now; several bases need the bounded list); the place itself is an SD World/Map place. Storage-at-a-base would need inventory to track where items are (not there today). Combat — yes as a lean data module: HP/stamina/conditions fields, foes as a bounded list cleared after the fight, SD dice checks for risky actions (v1.1 adapter: SD checks inside a dm root), simple rules (HP 0 → downed, damage cap per reply); the AI narrates. Trade-off vs RB: no engine, so outcomes are as fair as the dice/caps make them.

Lead: recommend A first as a data module on the bounded list — code owns the numbers (skill cost from the skill's entry, refused when short; HP/MP/SP floors; damage cap per reply; uses counted), the AI owns the story; current HP/MP/SP, known skills with costs and the foe list (≤ 3 active) go in the prompt; SD dice for risky moves. Lead's rule for the spec: `~ hurt` to 0 → downed (no per-reply damage cap); a death line is accepted only for a character already downed at the start of the reply (one reply to act: surrender, plead, potion, ally); companions/foes same rule; the death shows as a card; swipe/delete/Re-read undo it like any line.

- 28 Oct — USER: does it need a growing list at all? Let the player add rows/fields directly in the block; the AI sees them in the prompt and only updates them. Lead: agreed — much lighter. Rows are created only by reader actions (recorded like Drop), the narrator only updates rows that exist by their exact (folded) name; no narrator-created entries, so no name-matching/duplicates/creation-on-Re-read problems. Optional extra: the narrator may suggest a new row and the card offers an "Add" button (the "Make this a quest" pattern). Foes don't need rows: 3 fixed foe slots (fixed fields, fits v1). Still a small storage change (reader-named keys, a cap, e.g. 32), not the full growing-list feature.
- 28 Oct — USER asked for all the tricky parts to think about. Lead listed: reader edits vs swipe/delete (where an edit lives in history); rows added later vs Re-read of earlier replies; AI name drift; AI forgetting lines / writing lines that contradict the prose; refused costs after success was narrated; damage scaling (fixed amounts vs % of max HP) and foe health (wound steps, no HP); party (whose HP/skill: `| by:`); recovery/rests (RB's pain point: keep line-based, no time accounting); how a fight starts so the combat prompt appears; prompt size with many rows; changing a field's type mid-chat; Ersveil no-RB losses; story-only quests vs owed rewards.
- 28 Oct — USER decisions on the tricky parts: (1) reader edits are lost with a swipe/delete of the reply they sit on (no special handling); (2) refused cost → swipe; (3) missed line → tap fix on the card; (4) a match counts when the row's key phrase appears in the AI's phrase (prefix/suffix OK; lead: longest row name wins, case/hyphen/space folded); (5) fine (rows are reader-added); (6) damage as a percentage of max HP, severity decided by the narration; (7) foe health → separate discussion "B"; (8) combat prompt always on (no fight detection for the prompt); (9) recovery/rests → discussion B; (10) Skills screen with one card per character; (11) skills listed in the prompt only during a fight (fight = any foe slot filled); (12) field type changes → discussion B; (13) Ersveil drops its RB rules entirely and becomes an open-ended world pack again (no separate no-RB edition); (14) USER restarts chats from scratch (no migration concerns for existing chats).
- 28 Oct — USER: why numbers for HP/MP/SP at all if numbers are hard for the model? Use text descriptions; stats stay numbers mainly for dice (d20 + stat/2 vs foe). Lead: agreed — HP/MP/SP and foe health as ordered state words (enum fields data modules v1 already have; no arithmetic, no % damage); Down + the death rule stay; skills refused only when their resource is Empty; stats feed SD's existing `checks.roll` (d20 + modifier vs DC; success / success at a cost / fail). Open: opposed roll (foe d20 + tier bonus) vs fixed DC by foe tier; roll timing (SD `? check` told next reply vs Assist pre-roll before the reply); whether a skill use moves the resource a step. Rests and field-type changes get simpler (word steps). Spec agent redirected.

- 28 Oct — USER direction: (1) opposed roll, foe d20 + tier − PC level ("more dramatic"); asked if the 3-foe cap is still needed; (2) pre-call (Assist pre-roll) so the narrator can't cheat the outcome; (3) the AI decides resource use (a light spell costs little); (4) pack data modules (not new modules/extensions); PC roll = d20 + stat/2 + level [+ skill]. Lead: level would count twice (PC +lv and foe −lv) and raw level (to 90) swamps a d20 → proposed PC d20 + stat/2 + 2×class (Soul Class 1–9 = level band) + skill rank (Novice 0 … Master +3) vs foe d20 + 5 + 2×class; odds (PC class II, STR 14, Adept = +12): class I foe 70%, II 62%, IV 43%, VI 26%, VIII 14% (+~4% ties). Cap: no maths reason; keep fixed slots (fields) but 4–5 is fine, with groups as one slot ("Rats ×6"). Pre-roll: one extra small call per reply; swipe should re-tell the same roll (else swipe = reroll) — USER to confirm.

- 28 Oct — USER: combat card B (round log). Asked: whole fight in one reply or one round per reply (per-round seems costly)? Lead recommends a hybrid: each reply runs exchanges until a stop point (fight over; anyone on your side Down or Badly wounded; a foe flees/surrenders/is Down; a new wave; or a cap of ~5 exchanges); the pre-call rolls all of them up front (one pre-call per reply either way); the player's words can override ('fight it out' = to the end, 'carefully' = one exchange). Trivial fights end in one reply; dangerous ones pause at the moments that matter; fits the Down rule (death needs a reply between).
- 28 Oct — USER: MVU's combat feels more classic. Lead: two ways to get that: (1) classic LOOK with words (round labels, turn order, one coloured line per action, bars by steps) — cheap, current direction; (2) classic NUMBERS computed by code (HP/MP/SP numbers, damage = formula from stats + the d20, turn order by AGI, foe stat lines from tier tables, card prints 'Goblin A hits Rabbit for 12 damage'; the AI only narrates the log) — correct numbers unlike MVU, but a small fight engine (where RB grew heavy) and it reverses the words decision for HP/MP/SP. Asked USER to choose; lean engine rules if (2): one fixed formula, ≤ 5 foes, skills = cost + power, no status effects at first.

- 28 Oct — USER: ST has /roll — use it with the lean RPG maths? Lead (checked ST 1.19 source): built-in is the {{roll::1d20+12}} macro (rolls each time a prompt is built, so it re-rolls on swipe/regenerate); /roll is the official Dice extension (separate install). Interim: Quick Reply buttons can roll and post a fixed result with the player's message (no extra AI call, AI can't fake it; not undo-tracked by SB, stats kept by hand). Full design: dice are a tiny part of the code; the work is recording/undo, deciding stat/skill/target, and feeding the result before the reply. Idea to offer: a player-side Roll button (the player picks the skill) as an alternative to the AI pre-call — saves the extra call per reply.
- 28 Oct — USER: effort for an interim pack with reader-added skills, stats with a stat adder, and the pre-roll combat, with minimum checks? Interim total ≈ 2 days; built as early versions of R1/R2 pieces (hardened later, not thrown away). Risk of minimum checks: undo/replay edge cases less covered.
